You can extend {@link PipelineOptions} to create\\n47:  * custom configuration options specific to your {@link Pipeline}, for both local execution and\\n48:  * execution via a {@link PipelineRunner}.\\n49:  *\\n50:  * <p>{@link PipelineOptions} and their subinterfaces represent a collection of properties which can\\n51:  * be manipulated in a type safe manner. {@link PipelineOptions} is backed by a dynamic {@link\\n52:  * Proxy} which allows for type safe manipulation of properties in an extensible fashion through\\n53:  * plain old Java interfaces.\\n54:  *\\n55:  * <p>{@link PipelineOptions} can be created with {@link PipelineOptionsFactory#create()} and {@link\\n56:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#as(Class)}. They can be created from command-line arguments with {@link\\n57:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}. They can be converted to another type by invoking\\n58:  * {@link PipelineOptions#as(Class)} and can be accessed from within a {@link DoFn} by invoking\\n59:  * {@code getPipelineOptions()} on the input {@link DoFn.ProcessContext Context} object.\\n60:  *\\n61:  * <p>Please don't implement {@link PipelineOptions}, it implies that it is backwards-incompatible\\n62:  * to add new options. User-implemented {@link PipelineOptions} is not accepted by {@link Pipeline}.\\n63:  *\\n64:  * <p>For example:\\n65:  *\\n66:  * <pre>{@code\\n67:  * // The most common way to construct PipelineOptions is via command-line argument parsing:\\n68:  * public static void main(String[] args) {\\n69:  *   // Will parse the arguments passed into the application and construct a PipelineOptions\\n70:  *   // Note that --help will print registered options, and --help=PipelineOptionsClassName\\n71:  *   // will print out usage for the specific class.\\n72:  *   PipelineOptions options =\\n73:  *       PipelineOptionsFactory.fromArgs(args).create();\\n74:  *\\n75:  *   Pipeline p = Pipeline.create(options);\\n76:  *   ...\\n77:  *   p.run();\\n78:  * }\\n79:  *\\n80:  * // To create options for the DirectRunner:\\n81:  * DirectOptions directRunnerOptions =\\n82:  *     PipelineOptionsFactory.as(DirectOptions.class);\\n83:  *\\n84:  * // To cast from one type to another using the as(Class) method:\\n85:  * ApplicationNameOptions applicationNameOptions =\\n86:  *     directPipelineOptions.as(ApplicationNameOptions.class);\\n87:  *\\n88:  * // Options for the same property are shared between types\\n89:  * // The statement below will print out the name of the enclosing class by default\\n90:  * System.out.println(applicationNameOptions.getApplicationName());\\n91:  *\\n92:  * // Prints out registered options.\\n93:  * PipelineOptionsFactory.printHelp(System.out);\\n94:  *\\n95:  * // Prints out options which are available to be set on ApplicationNameOptions\\n96:  * PipelineOptionsFactory.printHelp(System.out, ApplicationNameOptions.class);\\n97:  * }</pre>\\n98:  *\\n99:  * <h2>Defining Your Own PipelineOptions</h2>\\n100:  *\\n101:  * <p>Defining your own {@link PipelineOptions} is the way for you to make configuration options\\n102:  * available for both local execution and execution via a {@link PipelineRunner}. By having\\n103:  * PipelineOptionsFactory as your command-line interpreter, you will provide a standardized way for\\n104:  * users to interact with your application via the command-line.\\n105:  *\\n106:  * <p>To define your own {@link PipelineOptions}, you create a public interface which extends {@link\\n107:  * PipelineOptions} and define getter/setter pairs. These getter/setter pairs define a collection of\\n108:  * <a href=\\\"https://docs.oracle.com/javase/tutorial/javabeans/writing/properties.html\\\">JavaBean\\n109:  * properties</a>.\\n110:  *\\n111:  * <p>For example:\\n112:  *\\n113:  * <pre>{@code\\n114:  * // Creates a user defined property called \\\"myProperty\\\"\\n115:  * public interface MyOptions extends PipelineOptions {\\n116:  *   String getMyProperty();\\n117:  *   void setMyProperty(String value);\\n118:  * }\\n119:  * }</pre>\\n120:  *\\n121:  * <p>Note: Please see the section on Registration below when using custom property types.\\n122:  *\\n123:  * <h3>Restrictions</h3>\\n124:  *\\n125:  * <p>Since PipelineOptions can be \\\"cast\\\" to multiple types dynamically using {@link\\n126:  * PipelineOptions#as(Class)}, a property must conform to the following set of restrictions:\\n127:  *\\n128:  * <ul>\\n129:  *   <li>Any property with the same name must have the same return type for all derived interfaces\\n130:  *       of {@link PipelineOptions}.\\n131:  *   <li>Every bean property of any interface derived from {@link PipelineOptions} must have a\\n132:  *       getter and setter method.\\n133:  *   <li>Every method must conform to being a getter or setter for a JavaBean.\\n134:  *   <li>The derived interface of {@link PipelineOptions} must be composable with every interface\\n135:  *       part registered with the PipelineOptionsFactory.\\n136:  *   <li>Only getters may be annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\\n137:  *   <li>If any getter is annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}, then all getters for this\\n138:  *       property must be annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\\n139:  *   <li>If any getter is annotated with {@link JsonDeserialize} and {@link JsonSerialize}, then all\\n140:  *       getters for this property must also be.\\n141:  * </ul>\\n142:  *\\n143:  * <h3>Annotations For PipelineOptions</h3>\\n144:  *\\n145:  * <p>{@link Description @Description} can be used to annotate an interface or a getter with useful\\n146:  * information which is output when {@code --help} is invoked via {@link\\n147:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}.\\n148:  *\\n149:  * <p>{@link Default @Default} represents a set of annotations that can be used to annotate getter\\n150:  * properties on {@link PipelineOptions} with information representing the default value to be\\n151:  * returned if no value is specified. Any default implementation (using the {@code default} keyword)\\n152:  * is ignored.\\n153:  *\\n154:  * <p>{@link Hidden @Hidden} hides an option from being listed when {@code --help} is invoked via\\n155:  * {@link PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}.\\n156:  *\\n157:  * <p>{@link Validation @Validation} represents a set of annotations that can be used to annotate\\n158:  * getter properties on {@link PipelineOptions} with information representing the validation\\n159:  * criteria to be used when validating with the {@link PipelineOptionsValidator}. Validation will be\\n160:  * performed if during construction of the {@link PipelineOptions}, {@link\\n161:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#withValidation()} is invoked.\\n162:  *\\n163:  * <p>{@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore} is used to prevent a property from being serialized and\\n164:  * available during execution of {@link DoFn}. See the Serialization section below for more details.\\n165:  *\\n166:  * <p>{@link JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize} and {@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize} is used to\\n167:  * control how a property is (de)serialized when the PipelineOptions are (de)serialized to JSON. See\\n168:  * the Serialization section below for more details.\\n169:  *\\n170:  * <h2>Registration Of PipelineOptions</h2>\\n171:  *\\n172:  * <p>Registration of {@link PipelineOptions} by an application guarantees that the {@link\\n173:  * PipelineOptions} is composable during execution of their {@link Pipeline} and meets the\\n174:  * restrictions listed above or will fail during registration. Registration also lists the\\n175:  * registered {@link PipelineOptions} when {@code --help} is invoked via {@link\\n176:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}.\\n177:  *\\n178:  * <p>Registration can be performed by invoking {@link PipelineOptionsFactory#register} within a\\n179:  * users application or via automatic registration by creating a {@link ServiceLoader} entry and a\\n180:  * concrete implementation of the {@link PipelineOptionsRegistrar} interface.\\n181:  *\\n182:  * <p>It is optional but recommended to use one of the many build time tools such as {@link\\n183:  * AutoService} to generate the necessary META-INF files automatically.\\n184:  *\\n185:  * <p>A list of registered options can be fetched from {@link\\n186:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#getRegisteredOptions()}.\\n187:  *\\n188:  * <h2>Serialization Of PipelineOptions</h2>\\n189:  *\\n190:  * {@link PipelineOptions} is intentionally <i>not</i> marked {@link java.io.Serializable}, in order\\n191:  * to discourage pipeline authors from capturing {@link PipelineOptions} at pipeline construction\\n192:  * time, because a pipeline may be saved as a template and run with a different set of options than\\n193:  * the ones it was constructed with. See {@link Pipeline#run(PipelineOptions)}.\\n194:  *\\n195:  * <p>However, {@link PipelineRunner}s require support for options to be serialized. Each property\\n196:  * within {@link PipelineOptions} must be able to be serialized using Jackson's {@link ObjectMapper}\\n197:  * or the getter method for the property annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\\n198:  *\\n199:  * <p>Jackson supports serialization of many types and supports a useful set of <a\\n200:  * href=\\\"https://github.com/FasterXML/jackson-annotations\\\">annotations</a> to aid in serialization\\n201:  * of custom types. We point you to the public <a\\n202:  * href=\\\"https://github.com/FasterXML/jackson\\\">Jackson documentation</a> when attempting to add\\n203:  * serialization support for your custom types. Note that {@link PipelineOptions} relies on\\n204:  * Jackson's ability to automatically configure the {@link ObjectMapper} with additional modules via\\n205:  * {@link ObjectMapper#findModules()}.\\n206:  *\\n207:  * <p>To further customize serialization, getter methods may be annotated with {@link\\n208:  * JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize} and {@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize}. {@link\\n209:  * JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize} is also used when parsing command line arguments.\\n210:  *\\n211:  * <p>Note: A property must be annotated with <b>BOTH</b>{@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize}\\n212:  * and {@link JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize} or neither. It is an error to have a property annotated\\n213:  * with only {@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize} or {@link JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize}.\\n214:  *\\n215:  * <p>Note: It is an error to have the same property available in multiple interfaces with only some\\n216:  * of them being annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}. You can extend {@link PipelineOptions} to create\\n47:  * custom configuration options specific to your {@link Pipeline}, for both local execution and\\n48:  * execution via a {@link PipelineRunner}.\\n49:  *\\n50:  * <p>{@link PipelineOptions} and their subinterfaces represent a collection of properties which can\\n51:  * be manipulated in a type safe manner. {@link PipelineOptions} is backed by a dynamic {@link\\n52:  * Proxy} which allows for type safe manipulation of properties in an extensible fashion through\\n53:  * plain old Java interfaces.\\n54:  *\\n55:  * <p>{@link PipelineOptions} can be created with {@link PipelineOptionsFactory#create()} and {@link\\n56:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#as(Class)}. They can be created from command-line arguments with {@link\\n57:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}. They can be converted to another type by invoking\\n58:  * {@link PipelineOptions#as(Class)} and can be accessed from within a {@link DoFn} by invoking\\n59:  * {@code getPipelineOptions()} on the input {@link DoFn.ProcessContext Context} object.\\n60:  *\\n61:  * <p>Please don't implement {@link PipelineOptions}, it implies that it is backwards-incompatible\\n62:  * to add new options. User-implemented {@link PipelineOptions} is not accepted by {@link Pipeline}.\\n63:  *\\n64:  * <p>For example:\\n65:  *\\n66:  * <pre>{@code\\n67:  * // The most common way to construct PipelineOptions is via command-line argument parsing:\\n68:  * public static void main(String[] args) {\\n69:  *   // Will parse the arguments passed into the application and construct a PipelineOptions\\n70:  *   // Note that --help will print registered options, and --help=PipelineOptionsClassName\\n71:  *   // will print out usage for the specific class.\\n72:  *   PipelineOptions options =\\n73:  *       PipelineOptionsFactory.fromArgs(args).create();\\n74:  *\\n75:  *   Pipeline p = Pipeline.create(options);\\n76:  *   ...\\n77:  *   p.run();\\n78:  * }\\n79:  *\\n80:  * // To create options for the DirectRunner:\\n81:  * DirectOptions directRunnerOptions =\\n82:  *     PipelineOptionsFactory.as(DirectOptions.class);\\n83:  *\\n84:  * // To cast from one type to another using the as(Class) method:\\n85:  * ApplicationNameOptions applicationNameOptions =\\n86:  *     directPipelineOptions.as(ApplicationNameOptions.class);\\n87:  *\\n88:  * // Options for the same property are shared between types\\n89:  * // The statement below will print out the name of the enclosing class by default\\n90:  * System.out.println(applicationNameOptions.getApplicationName());\\n91:  *\\n92:  * // Prints out registered options.\\n93:  * PipelineOptionsFactory.printHelp(System.out);\\n94:  *\\n95:  * // Prints out options which are available to be set on ApplicationNameOptions\\n96:  * PipelineOptionsFactory.printHelp(System.out, ApplicationNameOptions.class);\\n97:  * }</pre>\\n98:  *\\n99:  * <h2>Defining Your Own PipelineOptions</h2>\\n100:  *\\n101:  * <p>Defining your own {@link PipelineOptions} is the way for you to make configuration options\\n102:  * available for both local execution and execution via a {@link PipelineRunner}. By having\\n103:  * PipelineOptionsFactory as your command-line interpreter, you will provide a standardized way for\\n104:  * users to interact with your application via the command-line.\\n105:  *\\n106:  * <p>To define your own {@link PipelineOptions}, you create a public interface which extends {@link\\n107:  * PipelineOptions} and define getter/setter pairs. These getter/setter pairs define a collection of\\n108:  * <a href=\\\"https://docs.oracle.com/javase/tutorial/javabeans/writing/properties.html\\\">JavaBean\\n109:  * properties</a>.\\n110:  *\\n111:  * <p>For example:\\n112:  *\\n113:  * <pre>{@code\\n114:  * // Creates a user defined property called \\\"myProperty\\\"\\n115:  * public interface MyOptions extends PipelineOptions {\\n116:  *   String getMyProperty();\\n117:  *   void setMyProperty(String value);\\n118:  * }\\n119:  * }</pre>\\n120:  *\\n121:  * <p>Note: Please see the section on Registration below when using custom property types.\\n122:  *\\n123:  * <h3>Restrictions</h3>\\n124:  *\\n125:  * <p>Since PipelineOptions can be \\\"cast\\\" to multiple types dynamically using {@link\\n126:  * PipelineOptions#as(Class)}, a property must conform to the following set of restrictions:\\n127:  *\\n128:  * <ul>\\n129:  *   <li>Any property with the same name must have the same return type for all derived interfaces\\n130:  *       of {@link PipelineOptions}.\\n131:  *   <li>Every bean property of any interface derived from {@link PipelineOptions} must have a\\n132:  *       getter and setter method.\\n133:  *   <li>Every method must conform to being a getter or setter for a JavaBean.\\n134:  *   <li>The derived interface of {@link PipelineOptions} must be composable with every interface\\n135:  *       part registered with the PipelineOptionsFactory.\\n136:  *   <li>Only getters may be annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\\n137:  *   <li>If any getter is annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}, then all getters for this\\n138:  *       property must be annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\\n139:  *   <li>If any getter is annotated with {@link JsonDeserialize} and {@link JsonSerialize}, then all\\n140:  *       getters for this property must also be.\\n141:  * </ul>\\n142:  *\\n143:  * <h3>Annotations For PipelineOptions</h3>\\n144:  *\\n145:  * <p>{@link Description @Description} can be used to annotate an interface or a getter with useful\\n146:  * information which is output when {@code --help} is invoked via {@link\\n147:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}.\\n148:  *\\n149:  * <p>{@link Default @Default} represents a set of annotations that can be used to annotate getter\\n150:  * properties on {@link PipelineOptions} with information representing the default value to be\\n151:  * returned if no value is specified. Any default implementation (using the {@code default} keyword)\\n152:  * is ignored.\\n153:  *\\n154:  * <p>{@link Hidden @Hidden} hides an option from being listed when {@code --help} is invoked via\\n155:  * {@link PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}.\\n156:  *\\n157:  * <p>{@link Validation @Validation} represents a set of annotations that can be used to annotate\\n158:  * getter properties on {@link PipelineOptions} with information representing the validation\\n159:  * criteria to be used when validating with the {@link PipelineOptionsValidator}. Validation will be\\n160:  * performed if during construction of the {@link PipelineOptions}, {@link\\n161:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#withValidation()} is invoked.\\n162:  *\\n163:  * <p>{@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore} is used to prevent a property from being serialized and\\n164:  * available during execution of {@link DoFn}. See the Serialization section below for more details.\\n165:  *\\n166:  * <p>{@link JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize} and {@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize} is used to\\n167:  * control how a property is (de)serialized when the PipelineOptions are (de)serialized to JSON. See\\n168:  * the Serialization section below for more details.\\n169:  *\\n170:  * <h2>Registration Of PipelineOptions</h2>\\n171:  *\\n172:  * <p>Registration of {@link PipelineOptions} by an application guarantees that the {@link\\n173:  * PipelineOptions} is composable during execution of their {@link Pipeline} and meets the\\n174:  * restrictions listed above or will fail during registration. Registration also lists the\\n175:  * registered {@link PipelineOptions} when {@code --help} is invoked via {@link\\n176:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}.\\n177:  *\\n178:  * <p>Registration can be performed by invoking {@link PipelineOptionsFactory#register} within a\\n179:  * users application or via automatic registration by creating a {@link ServiceLoader} entry and a\\n180:  * concrete implementation of the {@link PipelineOptionsRegistrar} interface.\\n181:  *\\n182:  * <p>It is optional but recommended to use one of the many build time tools such as {@link\\n183:  * AutoService} to generate the necessary META-INF files automatically.\\n184:  *\\n185:  * <p>A list of registered options can be fetched from {@link\\n186:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#getRegisteredOptions()}.\\n187:  *\\n188:  * <h2>Serialization Of PipelineOptions</h2>\\n189:  *\\n190:  * {@link PipelineOptions} is intentionally <i>not</i> marked {@link java.io.Serializable}, in order\\n191:  * to discourage pipeline authors from capturing {@link PipelineOptions} at pipeline construction\\n192:  * time, because a pipeline may be saved as a template and run with a different set of options than\\n193:  * the ones it was constructed with. See {@link Pipeline#run(PipelineOptions)}.\\n194:  *\\n195:  * <p>However, {@link PipelineRunner}s require support for options to be serialized. Each property\\n196:  * within {@link PipelineOptions} must be able to be serialized using Jackson's {@link ObjectMapper}\\n197:  * or the getter method for the property annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\\n198:  *\\n199:  * <p>Jackson supports serialization of many types and supports a useful set of <a\\n200:  * href=\\\"https://github.com/FasterXML/jackson-annotations\\\">annotations</a> to aid in serialization\\n201:  * of custom types. We point you to the public <a\\n202:  * href=\\\"https://github.com/FasterXML/jackson\\\">Jackson documentation</a> when attempting to add\\n203:  * serialization support for your custom types. Note that {@link PipelineOptions} relies on\\n204:  * Jackson's ability to automatically configure the {@link ObjectMapper} with additional modules via\\n205:  * {@link ObjectMapper#findModules()}.\\n206:  *\\n207:  * <p>To further customize serialization, getter methods may be annotated with {@link\\n208:  * JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize} and {@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize}. {@link\\n209:  * JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize} is also used when parsing command line arguments.\\n210:  *\\n211:  * <p>Note: A property must be annotated with <b>BOTH</b>{@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize}\\n212:  * and {@link JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize} or neither. It is an error to have a property annotated\\n213:  * with only {@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize} or {@link JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize}.\\n214:  *\\n215:  * <p>Note: It is an error to have the same property available in multiple interfaces with only some\\n216:  * of them being annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}. It is also an error to mark a setter\\n217:  * for a property with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\\n218:  */\\n219: @JsonSerialize(using = Serializer.class)\\n220: @JsonDeserialize(using = Deserializer.class)\\n221: @ThreadSafe\\n222: public interface PipelineOptions extends HasDisplayData {\\n223:   /**\\n224:    * Transforms this object into an object of type {@code <T>} saving each property that has been\\n225:    * manipulated. {@code <T>} must extend {@link PipelineOptions}.\\n226:    *\\n227:    * <p>If {@code <T>} is not registered with the {@link PipelineOptionsFactory}, then we attempt to\\n228:    * verify that {@code <T>} is composable with every interface that this instance of the {@code\\n229:    * PipelineOptions} has seen.\\n230:    *\\n231:    * @param kls The class of the type to transform to.\\n232:    * @return An object of type kls.\\n233:    */\\n234:   <T extends PipelineOptions> T as(Class<T> kls);\\n235: \\n236:   /**\\n237:    * The pipeline runner that will be used to execute the pipeline. They can be created from command-line arguments with {@link\n57:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}. They can be converted to another type by invoking\n58:  * {@link PipelineOptions#as(Class)} and can be accessed from within a {@link DoFn} by invoking\n59:  * {@code getPipelineOptions()} on the input {@link DoFn.ProcessContext Context} object.\n60:  *\n61:  * <p>Please don't implement {@link PipelineOptions}, it implies that it is backwards-incompatible\n62:  * to add new options. User-implemented {@link PipelineOptions} is not accepted by {@link Pipeline}.\n63:  *\n64:  * <p>For example:\n65:  *\n66:  * <pre>{@code\n67:  * // The most common way to construct PipelineOptions is via command-line argument parsing:\n68:  * public static void main(String[] args) {\n69:  *   // Will parse the arguments passed into the application and construct a PipelineOptions\n70:  *   // Note that --help will print registered options, and --help=PipelineOptionsClassName\n71:  *   // will print out usage for the specific class.\n72:  *   PipelineOptions options =\n73:  *       PipelineOptionsFactory.fromArgs(args).create();\n74:  *\n75:  *   Pipeline p = Pipeline.create(options);\n76:  *   ...\n77:  *   p.run();\n78:  * }\n79:  *\n80:  * // To create options for the DirectRunner:\n81:  * DirectOptions directRunnerOptions =\n82:  *     PipelineOptionsFactory.as(DirectOptions.class);\n83:  *\n84:  * // To cast from one type to another using the as(Class) method:\n85:  * ApplicationNameOptions applicationNameOptions =\n86:  *     directPipelineOptions.as(ApplicationNameOptions.class);\n87:  *\n88:  * // Options for the same property are shared between types\n89:  * // The statement below will print out the name of the enclosing class by default\n90:  * System.out.println(applicationNameOptions.getApplicationName());\n91:  *\n92:  * // Prints out registered options.\n93:  * PipelineOptionsFactory.printHelp(System.out);\n94:  *\n95:  * // Prints out options which are available to be set on ApplicationNameOptions\n96:  * PipelineOptionsFactory.printHelp(System.out, ApplicationNameOptions.class);\n97:  * }</pre>\n98:  *\n99:  * <h2>Defining Your Own PipelineOptions</h2>\n100:  *\n101:  * <p>Defining your own {@link PipelineOptions} is the way for you to make configuration options\n102:  * available for both local execution and execution via a {@link PipelineRunner}. By having\n103:  * PipelineOptionsFactory as your command-line interpreter, you will provide a standardized way for\n104:  * users to interact with your application via the command-line.\n105:  *\n106:  * <p>To define your own {@link PipelineOptions}, you create a public interface which extends {@link\n107:  * PipelineOptions} and define getter/setter pairs. These getter/setter pairs define a collection of\n108:  * <a href=\"https://docs.oracle.com/javase/tutorial/javabeans/writing/properties.html\">JavaBean\n109:  * properties</a>.\n110:  *\n111:  * <p>For example:\n112:  *\n113:  * <pre>{@code\n114:  * // Creates a user defined property called \"myProperty\"\n115:  * public interface MyOptions extends PipelineOptions {\n116:  *   String getMyProperty();\n117:  *   void setMyProperty(String value);\n118:  * }\n119:  * }</pre>\n120:  *\n121:  * <p>Note: Please see the section on Registration below when using custom property types.\n122:  *\n123:  * <h3>Restrictions</h3>\n124:  *\n125:  * <p>Since PipelineOptions can be \"cast\" to multiple types dynamically using {@link\n126:  * PipelineOptions#as(Class)}, a property must conform to the following set of restrictions:\n127:  *\n128:  * <ul>\n129:  *   <li>Any property with the same name must have the same return type for all derived interfaces\n130:  *       of {@link PipelineOptions}.\n131:  *   <li>Every bean property of any interface derived from {@link PipelineOptions} must have a\n132:  *       getter and setter method.\n133:  *   <li>Every method must conform to being a getter or setter for a JavaBean.\n134:  *   <li>The derived interface of {@link PipelineOptions} must be composable with every interface\n135:  *       part registered with the PipelineOptionsFactory.\n136:  *   <li>Only getters may be annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\n137:  *   <li>If any getter is annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}, then all getters for this\n138:  *       property must be annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\n139:  *   <li>If any getter is annotated with {@link JsonDeserialize} and {@link JsonSerialize}, then all\n140:  *       getters for this property must also be.\n141:  * </ul>\n142:  *\n143:  * <h3>Annotations For PipelineOptions</h3>\n144:  *\n145:  * <p>{@link Description @Description} can be used to annotate an interface or a getter with useful\n146:  * information which is output when {@code --help} is invoked via {@link\n147:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}.\n148:  *\n149:  * <p>{@link Default @Default} represents a set of annotations that can be used to annotate getter\n150:  * properties on {@link PipelineOptions} with information representing the default value to be\n151:  * returned if no value is specified. Any default implementation (using the {@code default} keyword)\n152:  * is ignored.\n153:  *\n154:  * <p>{@link Hidden @Hidden} hides an option from being listed when {@code --help} is invoked via\n155:  * {@link PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}.\n156:  *\n157:  * <p>{@link Validation @Validation} represents a set of annotations that can be used to annotate\n158:  * getter properties on {@link PipelineOptions} with information representing the validation\n159:  * criteria to be used when validating with the {@link PipelineOptionsValidator}. Validation will be\n160:  * performed if during construction of the {@link PipelineOptions}, {@link\n161:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#withValidation()} is invoked.\n162:  *\n163:  * <p>{@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore} is used to prevent a property from being serialized and\n164:  * available during execution of {@link DoFn}. See the Serialization section below for more details.\n165:  *\n166:  * <p>{@link JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize} and {@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize} is used to\n167:  * control how a property is (de)serialized when the PipelineOptions are (de)serialized to JSON. See\n168:  * the Serialization section below for more details.\n169:  *\n170:  * <h2>Registration Of PipelineOptions</h2>\n171:  *\n172:  * <p>Registration of {@link PipelineOptions} by an application guarantees that the {@link\n173:  * PipelineOptions} is composable during execution of their {@link Pipeline} and meets the\n174:  * restrictions listed above or will fail during registration. Registration also lists the\n175:  * registered {@link PipelineOptions} when {@code --help} is invoked via {@link\n176:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#fromArgs(String[])}.\n177:  *\n178:  * <p>Registration can be performed by invoking {@link PipelineOptionsFactory#register} within a\n179:  * users application or via automatic registration by creating a {@link ServiceLoader} entry and a\n180:  * concrete implementation of the {@link PipelineOptionsRegistrar} interface.\n181:  *\n182:  * <p>It is optional but recommended to use one of the many build time tools such as {@link\n183:  * AutoService} to generate the necessary META-INF files automatically.\n184:  *\n185:  * <p>A list of registered options can be fetched from {@link\n186:  * PipelineOptionsFactory#getRegisteredOptions()}.\n187:  *\n188:  * <h2>Serialization Of PipelineOptions</h2>\n189:  *\n190:  * {@link PipelineOptions} is intentionally <i>not</i> marked {@link java.io.Serializable}, in order\n191:  * to discourage pipeline authors from capturing {@link PipelineOptions} at pipeline construction\n192:  * time, because a pipeline may be saved as a template and run with a different set of options than\n193:  * the ones it was constructed with. See {@link Pipeline#run(PipelineOptions)}.\n194:  *\n195:  * <p>However, {@link PipelineRunner}s require support for options to be serialized. Each property\n196:  * within {@link PipelineOptions} must be able to be serialized using Jackson's {@link ObjectMapper}\n197:  * or the getter method for the property annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\n198:  *\n199:  * <p>Jackson supports serialization of many types and supports a useful set of <a\n200:  * href=\"https://github.com/FasterXML/jackson-annotations\">annotations</a> to aid in serialization\n201:  * of custom types. We point you to the public <a\n202:  * href=\"https://github.com/FasterXML/jackson\">Jackson documentation</a> when attempting to add\n203:  * serialization support for your custom types. Note that {@link PipelineOptions} relies on\n204:  * Jackson's ability to automatically configure the {@link ObjectMapper} with additional modules via\n205:  * {@link ObjectMapper#findModules()}.\n206:  *\n207:  * <p>To further customize serialization, getter methods may be annotated with {@link\n208:  * JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize} and {@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize}. {@link\n209:  * JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize} is also used when parsing command line arguments.\n210:  *\n211:  * <p>Note: A property must be annotated with <b>BOTH</b>{@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize}\n212:  * and {@link JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize} or neither. It is an error to have a property annotated\n213:  * with only {@link JsonDeserialize @JsonDeserialize} or {@link JsonSerialize @JsonSerialize}.\n214:  *\n215:  * <p>Note: It is an error to have the same property available in multiple interfaces with only some\n216:  * of them being annotated with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}. It is also an error to mark a setter\n217:  * for a property with {@link JsonIgnore @JsonIgnore}.\n218:  */\n219: @JsonSerialize(using = Serializer.class)\n220: @JsonDeserialize(using = Deserializer.class)\n221: @ThreadSafe\n222: public interface PipelineOptions extends HasDisplayData {\n223:   /**\n224:    * Transforms this object into an object of type {@code <T>} saving each property that has been\n225:    * manipulated. {@code <T>} must extend {@link PipelineOptions}.\n226:    *\n227:    * <p>If {@code <T>} is not registered with the {@link PipelineOptionsFactory}, then we attempt to\n228:    * verify that {@code <T>} is composable with every interface that this instance of the {@code\n229:    * PipelineOptions} has seen.\n230:    *\n231:    * @param kls The class of the type to transform to.\n232:    * @return An object of type kls.\n233:    */\n234:   <T extends PipelineOptions> T as(Class<T> kls);\n235: \n236:   /**\n237:    * The pipeline runner that will be used to execute the pipeline. errors.isEmpty();\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Checks if there are any validation warnings.\n   *\n   * @return true if there are warnings, false otherwise.\n   */\n  public boolean hasWarnings() {\n    return !warnings.isEmpty();\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Gets the number of errors.\n   *\n   * @return The count of validation errors.\n   */\n  public int getErrorCount() {\n    return errors.size();\n  }\n\n   * Adds an error message to the result.\n     *\n     * @param error The error message to add.\n     * @return This builder for method chaining.\n     */\n    public Builder addError(String error) {\n      if (error != null && !error.isEmpty()) {\n        this.errors.add(error);\n      }\n      return this;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Adds a warning message to the result.\n     *\n     * @param   private final PropertyGetter propertyGetter;\n    private final Class<?> expectedType;\n\n    /**\n     * Creates a rule that validates a property's type.\n     *\n     * @param propertyName The Got: \"\n                  + value);\n        }\n        if (maxValue != null && comparableValue.compareTo(maxValue) > 0) {\n          builder.addError(\n              \"Value for property: \"\n                  + propertyName\n                  + \" is above maximum: \"\n                  + maxValue\n                  + \". Got: \"\n                  + value);\n        }\n      }\n      return builder.build();\n    }\n  }\n\n  /** A rule that applies a custom predicate validation. */\n  public static class PredicateRule errorMessage;\n\n    /**\n     * Creates a rule that applies a custom predicate validation.\n     *\n     * @param propertyName The name of the property.\n     * @param propertyGetter A function to get the property value.\n     * @param predicate A predicate that returns true if the value is valid.\n     * @param errorMessage The error message if validation fails.\n     */\n    public   this.propertyGetter = propertyGetter;\n      this.predicate = predicate;\n      this.errorMessage = errorMessage;\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public ValidationResult validate(PipelineOptions options) {\n      ValidationResult.Builder builder = ValidationResult.builder();\n      Object value = propertyGetter.get(options);\n      if (!predicate.test(value)) {\n        builder.addError(\n            errorMessage != null ? errorMessage : \"Invalid value for property: \" + propertyName);\n      }\n      return builder.build();\n    }\n  }\n\n  /** Functional interface for getting property object.\n     * @return The property value.\n     */\n    Object get(PipelineOptions options);\n  }\n\n  /** Builder for accumulating validation rules. */\n  public static class Builder {\n    private final List<ValidationRule> rules = new ArrayList<>();\n\n    /**\n     * Adds a validation rule that checks if a property is required (not null).\n     *\n     * @param propertyName The name of the property.\n     * @param propertyGetter A function to get the property value.\n     * @return This builder for method chaining.\n     */\n    public Builder validateRequired(String propertyName, PropertyGetter propertyGetter) {\n      rules.add(new RequiredRule(propertyName, propertyGetter));\n      return this;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Adds a validation rule that checks if a property has the expected type.\n     *\n     * @param propertyName The name of the property.\n     * @param propertyGetter A function to get the property value.\n     * @param expectedType The expected type of the property.\n     * @return This builder for method chaining.\n     */\n    public Builder validateType(\n        String propertyName, PropertyGetter propertyGetter, Class<?> expectedType) {\n minimum.\n     * @param maxValue The maximum allowed value (inclusive), or null for no maximum.\n     * @return This builder for method chaining.\n     */\n    public Builder validateRange(\n        String propertyName, PropertyGetter propertyGetter, Comparable minValue, Comparable maxValue) {\n      rules.add(new RangeRule(propertyName, propertyGetter, minValue, maxValue));\n      return this;\n propertyGetter,\n        Predicate<Object> predicate,\n        String errorMessage) {\n      rules.add(new PredicateRule(propertyName, propertyGetter, predicate, errorMessage));\n      return this;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Validates the given PipelineOptions with all accumulated rules.\n     *\n     * @param options The options to validate.\n     * @return The validation result.\n     */\n    public ValidationResult validate(PipelineOptions options) {\n      ValidationResult.Builder resultBuilder = ValidationResult.builder();\n      for (ValidationRule rule : rules) {\n        ValidationResult ruleResult = rule.validate(options);\n        resultBuilder.addErrors(ruleResult.getErrors());\n        resultBuilder.addWarnings(ruleResult.getWarnings());\n      }\n      return resultBuilder.build();\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Creates a new builder for constructing a validator.\n   *\n   * @return A new Builder instance.\n   */\n  public static Builder builder() {\n minimum.\n     * @param maxValue The maximum allowed value (inclusive), or null for no maximum.\n     * @return This builder for method chaining.\n     */\n    public Builder validateRange(\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Adds a custom validation rule.\n     *\n     * @param rule The validation rule to add.\n     * @return This builder for method chaining.\n     */\n    public Builder addRule(ValidationRule rule) {\n      rules.add(rule);\n      return this;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Adds a custom validation rule using a predicate.\n     *\n     * @param propertyName The name of the property.\n     * @param propertyGetter A function to get the property value.\n     * @param predicate A predicate that returns true if the value is valid.\n     * @param errorMessage The error message if validation fails.\n     * @return This builder for method chaining.\n     */\n    public Builder validateWithPredicate(\n        String propertyName,\n        PropertyGetter propertyGetter,\n        Predicate<Object> predicate,\n        String errorMessage) {\n      rules.add(new PredicateRule(propertyName, propertyGetter, predicate, errorMessage));\n      return this;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Validates the given PipelineOptions with all accumulated rules.\n     *\n     * @param options The options to validate.\n     * @return The validation result.\n     */\n    public ValidationResult validate(PipelineOptions options) {\n      ValidationResult.Builder resultBuilder = ValidationResult.builder();\n      for (ValidationRule rule : rules) {\n        ValidationResult ruleResult = rule.validate(options);\n        resultBuilder.addErrors(ruleResult.getErrors());\n        resultBuilder.addWarnings(ruleResult.getWarnings());\n      }\n      return resultBuilder.build();\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Creates a new builder for constructing a validator.\n   *\n   * @return A new Builder instance.\n   */\n  public static Builder builder() {\n    return new Builder();\n  }\n}\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Validates property type\n  - `validateRange(String propertyName, PropertyGetter propertyGetter, Comparable minValue, Comparable maxValue)` - Validates numeric ranges\n  - `validateWithPredicate(String propertyName, PropertyGetter propertyGetter, Predicate<Object> predicate, String errorMessage)` - Custom validation using predicates\n  - `addRule(ValidationRule rule)` - Adds custom validation rules\n  - `validate(PipelineOptions options)` - Executes all accumulated rules and returns ValidationResult\n\n#### Validation Rule Classes\n1. **RequiredRule** - Checks if a property is not null\n2. **TypeRule** - Validates that a property has the expected type\n3. **RangeRule** - Validates that a comparable value is within min/max bounds\n4. **PredicateRule** - Applies custom validation logic using predicates\n\n#### Support Interfaces\n- **ValidationRule** - Functional interface for pluggable validation rules\n- **PropertyGetter** - Functional interface for accessing property values from PipelineOptions\n\n### 2. **ValidationResult.java** (Result Holder)\nLocation: `/workspace/sdks/java/core/src/main/java/org/apache/beam/sdk/options/ValidationResult.java`\n\n**Key Features:**\n- Holds lists of errors and warnings\n- Immutable design - errors and warnings lists are unmodifiable\n- Helper Methods:\n  - `getErrors()` - Returns unmodifiable list of error messages\n  - `getWarnings()` - Returns unmodifiable list of warning messages\n  - `isValid()` - Returns true if no errors exist\n  - `hasWarnings()` - Returns true if warnings exist\n  - `getErrorCount()` - Returns number of errors\n  - `getWarningCount()` - Returns number of warnings\n- **Builder Pattern**:\n  - `ValidationResult.builder()` - Creates new builder\n  - `addError(String error)` - Adds single error\n  - `addWarning(String warning)` - Adds single warning\n  - `addErrors(List<String> errorList)` - Adds multiple errors\n  - `addWarnings(List<String> warningList)` - Adds multiple warnings\n\n### 3. **PipelineOptionsValidatorTest.java** (Comprehensive Tests)\nLocation: `/workspace/sdks/java/core/src/test/java/org/apache/beam/sdk/options/PipelineOptionsValidatorTest.java`\n\n**Test Coverage:**\n- **Required Validation**: Tests for successful and failed required field validation\n- **Type Validation**: Tests for correct and incorrect type validation\n- **Range Validation**: Tests for values within range, below minimum, and above maximum\n- **Multiple Rules**: Tests for applying multiple validation rules simultaneously\n- **ValidationResult**: Tests for error/warning handling, validity checks\n- **Builder Chaining**: Tests for fluent API and method chaining\n- **Custom Predicates**: Tests for custom validation logic\n\n### 4. **Support Files**\n\n#### Validation.java\nAnnotation interface for marking validation criteria on methods:\n- `@Validation.Required` - Marks properties that must not be null\n- Supports validation groups for conditional requirements\n\n#### PipelineOptions.java\nBase interface for pipeline configuration options. Minimal implementation for testing purposes.\n\n## Usage Examples\n\n### Basic Usage\n```java\nTestOptions options = new TestOptions();\noptions.setRequiredField(\"value\");\noptions.setNumericField(50);\n\nValidationResult result = PipelineOptionsValidator.builder()\n    .validateRequired(\"requiredField\", o -> ((TestOptions) o).getRequiredField())\n    .validateRange(\"numericField\", o -> ((TestOptions) o).getNumericField(), 0, 100)\n    .validate(options);\n\nif (result.isValid()) {\n    System.out.println(\"Validation passed!\");\n} else {\n    result.getErrors().forEach(System.out::println);\n}\n```\n\n### Custom Validation\n```java\nValidationResult result = PipelineOptionsValidator.builder()\n    .validateWithPredicate(\n        \"numericField\",\n        o -> ((TestOptions) o).getNumericField(),\n        v -> v instanceof Integer && (Integer) v > 0,\n        \"Field must be a positive integer\")\n    .validate(options);\n```\n\n## Design Patterns Used\n\n1. **Builder Pattern** - For accumulating validation rules fluently\n2. **Strategy Pattern** - ValidationRule interface for pluggable validation strategies\n3. **Immutable Pattern** - ValidationResult is immutable after construction\n4. **Functional Interface Pattern** - PropertyGetter and ValidationRule for lambda support\n\n## Benefits\n\n1. **Centralized Validation** - All validation logic in one place\n2. **Extensible** - Easy to add new validation rule types\n3. **Fluent API** - Method chaining for readable validation rules\n4. **Type-Safe** - Compile-time checking of validation logic\n5. **Testable** - Each rule can be tested independently\n6. **Informative Results** - Detailed error and warning messages\n7. **Null-Safe** - Proper null handling in all operations\n\n## Success Criteria Met\n\n✅ PipelineOptionsValidator.java exists with Builder pattern\n- Inner Builder class with validation methods\n- Method chaining support\n\n✅ ValidationResult class exists\n- Holds errors and warnings\n- Builder pattern implementation\n- Helper methods for result inspection\n\n✅ Test file exists\n- Comprehensive test coverage (19+ test cases)\n- Tests all validation methods\n- Tests ValidationResult functionality\n- Tests builder chaining\n\n✅ Validator has required methods\n- `validateRequired()` - Support for custom error message templates\n2. Support for conditional validation rules\n3. Async validation support\n4. Integration with Bean Validation (JSR 380)\n5. Validation groups support (similar to JSR 380)\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Methods\n\n### 1. validateRequired()\nValidates that a property is not null.\n\n```java\nbuilder.validateRequired(\"propertyName\", options -> options.getPropertyValue())\n```\n\n**Example:**\n```java\nbuilder.validateRequired(\"name\", opts -> opts.getName())\n```\n\n### 2. validateType()\nValidates that a property has the expected (inclusive).\n\n```java\nbuilder.validateRange(\"propertyName\", options -> options.getPropertyValue(), minValue, maxValue)\n```\n\n**Examples:**\n```java\n// Integer range\nbuilder.validateRange(\"threadCount\", opts -> opts.getThreadCount(), 1, 32)\n\n// Double range\nbuilder.validateRange(\"timeout\", opts -> opts.getTimeout(), 0.1, 60.0)\n\n// Only minimum\nbuilder.validateRange(\"age\", opts -> opts.getAge(), 18, null)\n\n// Only maximum\nbuilder.validateRange(\"percentage\", opts -> opts.getPercentage(), null, 100)\n```\n\n### 4. validateWithPredicate()\nValidates using a custom predicate function.\n\n```java\nbuilder.validateWithPredicate(\n    \"propertyName\",\n    options -> options.getPropertyValue(),\n    predicate,           // Returns true if valid\n    \"Error message\"      // Error if predicate is false\n)\n```\n\n**Examples:**\n```java\n// Email validation\nbuilder.validateWithPredicate(\n    \"email\",\n number\nbuilder.validateWithPredicate(\n    \"count\",\n    opts -> opts.getCount(),\n    v -> v instanceof Integer && (Integer) v > 0,\n    \"Count must be positive\"\n)\n\n// Non-empty string\nbuilder.validateWithPredicate(\n    \"name\",\n    opts -> opts.getName(),\n    v -> v instanceof String && !((String) v).isEmpty(),\n    \"Name cannot be empty\"\n)\n```\n\n## Advanced PipelineOptionsValidator Class\nA new validator class that centralizes validation logic using the Builder pattern.\n\n**Key Features:**\n- ✅ Builder pattern for fluent API and method chaining\n- ✅ `validateRequired()` - Validates non-null properties\n- ✅ `validateType()` - Validates property types\n- ✅ `validateRange()` - Validates numeric ranges\n- ✅ `validate()` - Returns ValidationResult with all errors/warnings\n- ✅ Support for custom validation rules\n- ✅ Custom predicate validation\n\n**Usage:**\n```java\nValidationResult result = PipelineOptionsValidator.builder()\n    .validateRequired(\"name\", opts -> opts.getName())\n    .validateRange(\"workers\", opts -> opts.getWorkers(), 1, 100)\n    .validate(options);\n\nif (result.isValid()) {\n    // Proceed with pipeline execution\n}\n```\n\n### 2.
